\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}

\hskip 20.00003pt\updownarrow
(
89
)

\hskip 20.00003pt\updownarrow
(
96
)

(structure equations).

\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}

\hskip 1.00006pt{\longleftrightarrow}^{\hskip-9.01396pt{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}}\hskip 5.50003pt

\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}

We use short-hand representations such that
{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})=\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
and
{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}{\circ}{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})={\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
.
A covariant differential in the dual bundle is provided as

\displaystyle\hat{d}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\mathfrak{a}}

\displaystyle:={\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\mathfrak{a}}-i\hskip 1.00006pt{{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt[\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}{\mathfrak{a}}]_{\wedge}

for
{\mathfrak{a}}\in\Omega^{p}
, and Bianchi identities in the dual bundle are

\displaystyle\left(\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\right)^{2}=-i{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}},~~\textrm{and}~~\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}=0.

(97)

Dual curvature in spnior bundle:

We defined the curvature and the connection in the dual inertial bundle in section
3.1.2
; the dual spinor-connection is obtained using the dual spin-connection and the
Spin(3,1)
generator.
The dual spinor connection is defined as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}:=-\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta_{\circ\circ}\eta_{\star\star}[\hat{\mathfrak{w}}]^{{\circ}\star}{S}^{{\circ}\star}.

The dual spinor-curvature is provided for given
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}
using the structure equation as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{~}

\displaystyle:=d\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{~}+{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{~}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{~},

where the dual curvature fulfils
{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{~})=\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{~}
.

Spinor valued section
\hat{\psi}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{}M},{\mathcal{}S}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{SU(N)})
is introduced also in the dual space.
We define an action of the Hodge-dual operator on sections as

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}})={{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}~~\textrm{and}~~{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}})={{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}.

Remark
3.5
is maintained also for
\hat{\psi}
.
The Dirac conjugate of
\psi
and
\hat{\psi}
is denoted as
\bar{\hat{\psi}}
and
\bar{\hat{\psi}}
, whose components are a Dirac conjugate of each element and is homomorphic with respect to dual and its adjoint operators as follows:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}})=\overline{{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}})}={\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}},~~\textrm{and}~~{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}})=\overline{{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}({\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}})}={\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\psi}$}}.

Kramers pairs for the spinor sections are provided as

\displaystyle{K\hskip-1.99997ptP}\left(\psi\right):=\left(\psi,\hat{\psi}\right)~~\textrm{and}~~{K\hskip-1.99997ptP}\left(\bar{\psi}\right):=\left(\bar{\psi},\bar{\hat{\psi}}\right).

Dual curvature in spnior-gauge bundle:

The spinor- and gauge connections and their Hodge-dual belong to the same bundle as
remark
2.7
.
They are provided as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}:=\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}+{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}},~~\textrm{and}~~\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}:=\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}+{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}},

and construct Kramers pairs
{K\hskip-1.99997ptP}({\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}^{~})
and
{K\hskip-1.99997ptP}(\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}^{~})
.

The covariant differential and the dual Dirac operator are provided as

\displaystyle{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{\mathfrak{a}}

\displaystyle:=d{\mathfrak{a}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}+{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\left[\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt},{\mathfrak{a}}\right]_{\wedge}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}+{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes\left(-i\hskip 1.00006pt{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt[\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}},{\mathfrak{a}}]_{\wedge}\right),

\displaystyle{\not\!\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}

\displaystyle:=\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}(d-i{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\mathfrak{w}}\cdot{{S}}){{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}-{i}{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota^{~}_{\gamma_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\otimes{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}},

where
{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}=(\hat{\psi}^{1},\hat{\psi}^{2},\cdots,\hat{\psi}^{N})^{T}
, and
{\mathfrak{a}}\in\Omega^{p}
and
{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\in{\mathcal{}S}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\otimes N}\otimes{SU(N)}
.
The dual- and adjoint operators are homomorphic as follows:

\begin{array}[]{cclccl}{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}\left({{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}\right)&={\not\!\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}},&{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}\left({\not\!\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\right)&={\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}.\end{array}

The existence of the dual spinor that provides the dual curvature as a solution of the equation of motion is not trivial and is discussed in
Appendix
A
.

3.3
Yang–Mills bundle

The gauge-spinor bundle is referred to as the
Yang–Mills bundle
when it is represented using the
({2\hskip-1.00006pt\times\hskip-1.00006pt2})
-matrix.
The connection and the curvature of the Yang–Mills bundle are defined in the secondary superspace as

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:={\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}+{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt},~~\textrm{where}~~{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}&{0}\\
{0}&\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}\end{array}\right),~~{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}&{0}\\
{0}&\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\end{array}\right).

The Yang–Mills curvature is provided using the structure equation such that:

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{F}$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}

\displaystyle:=d{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}\wedge{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}+{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\hskip-0.81949pt}\otimes(-ic^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\wedge{\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}),

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}&0\\
0&\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\end{array}\right).

The dual operator in the Yang–Mills bundle is introduced as

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}={{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Gamma\gamma{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}={{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}\\
{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}&0\end{array}\right)=-{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{-1}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}},

that acts on the curvature as

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\left(\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\right):={\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{-1}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}\\
\end{array}\right).

Dual operator
{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}
acts on the Yang–Mills connection formally as defined in section
2.4.2
.
A structure equation are homomorphic such that:

{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}

\hskip 5.0pt\left(\overset{{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.73616pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.73616pt\textrm{M}}}}{\longleftrightarrow}\right)\hskip 5.50003pt

{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\right)

\hskip 20.00003pt\updownarrow
(
3.3
)

\hskip 10.00002pt\updownarrow

(structure equations).

\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}

\overset{{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.73616pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.73616pt\textrm{M}}}}{\longleftrightarrow}\hskip 5.50003pt

{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{-1}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}

that is apparent owing to relations

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{X}$}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{-1}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}={\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{X}$}}\bigl|_{\bullet\leftrightarrow\hskip 0.81949pt\hat{\bullet}},

\displaystyle~~\textrm{for}~~{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{X}$}}={\mbox{\boldmath${\mathfrak{A}}$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}~\textrm{or}~~{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{F}$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}.

A covariant differential in the Yang–Mills bundle is

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{}D}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}&0\\
0&{\not\!\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\\
\end{array}\right).

The covariant differential is compatible to the dual operator such as

{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}({{\mathcal{}D}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}})={{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{{\mathcal{}D}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\hskip 1.00006pt{{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}^{-1}_{\hskip-0.81949pt{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}}={{\mathcal{}D}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\bigl|_{d_{\hskip-1.47221pts\hskip-0.73616ptg}\leftrightarrow\hat{d}_{{\hskip-1.47221pts\hskip-0.73616ptg}}},

and the dual operator preserves the Bianchi identities.

The Clifford algebra and Dirac operator are introduced to the Yang–Mills bundle as follows:
One-dimensional Clifford algebra represented as

\displaystyle\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:={\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\hskip 1.99997pt\gamma,~~

\displaystyle\textrm{where}~~{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:={\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}.

and corresponding chiral and projection operators are, respectively, provided as

\displaystyle\Gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:={\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\hskip 1.99997pt\Gamma~~\textrm{and}~~P^{\pm}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:=\frac{{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\pm\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}{2}.

(100)

They fulfil relations
\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\Gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}+\Gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}=0
and
\Gamma^{2}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}=\gamma^{2}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}={\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}
.
The Yang–Mills form is defined using the Clifford algebra as follows:

Definition 3.11

(Yang–Mills form)
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{ym}

\displaystyle:=\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\hat{\mathfrak{F}}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}^{~}\\
\mathfrak{F}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}^{~}&0\end{array}\right)\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}.

The secondary superspace is induced on a vector space
\{\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\}/G_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\otimes\{\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\}/G_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
owing to chiral operator
\Gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}
.
Clifford algebra
\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}
indices the Dirac operator such that:

\displaystyle{\not\!\mathcal{}D}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}

\displaystyle:=\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}{{\mathcal{}D}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\\
{\not\!\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}&0\\
\end{array}\right).

The Dirac spinor defined over the Yang–Mills bundle is defined as

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}\\
{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\end{array}\right)~

\displaystyle\textrm{yielding}~~{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}\left({\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{r}-{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\\
{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}\end{array}\right).

The Dirac conjugate of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}
and its dual in this representation are provided as

\displaystyle\overline{{\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}~:=~{\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}^{\dagger}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\left(\gamma^{0}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\gamma^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left({\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}^{\dagger},{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}^{\dagger}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mbox{\boldmath$0$}}&\gamma^{0}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\\
\gamma^{0}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}&{\mbox{\boldmath$0$}}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\bar{{{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}}},\bar{{{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}}\right),

and

\displaystyle{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}\left(\overline{{\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\right)=\left(\bar{{{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}}},-\bar{{{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}}\right).

4
Lagrangian and equation of motion

In classical mechanics, equations of motion are extracted from the action integral using the variational method with appropriate boundary conditions.
The Lagrangian is a four-form object consisting of connections, curvatures, sections, and invariant differentials in principal bundles.
This section introduces three fundamental Lagrangian, i.e., Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian for gravity, matter-field and gauge-filed Lagrangians for the Yang–Mills theory with the Lorentzian metric for simplicity.
Then,we extract equations of motion from the Lagrangian.
Extension to the
\theta
-metric is realized by replacing
{\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}\mapsto{\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}_{\theta}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath$\epsilon$}}\mapsto{\mbox{\boldmath$\epsilon$}}_{\theta}
,
{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}\mapsto{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}}}
, and
\mathfrak{v}\mapsto\mathfrak{v}_{\theta}
.

4.1
Einstein–Hilbert action

The gravitational Lagrangian four-form consists of curvature
\mathfrak{R}
and section
{\mathfrak{e}}
in the co-Poincaré bundle; the definition is given as follows
[
33
,
34
]
:

Definition 4.1

(Gravitational Lagrngian)
.

The gravitational Lagrangian and the corresponding action integral are defined as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{Gr}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa}\left(\frac{1}{2}Str\left[{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{F}$}}_{Gr}\wedge{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathfrak{F}$}}_{Gr}\right]-\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathfrak{v}_{\theta}\right),

(101)

where
\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}
is the cosmological constant.
Integration of the Lagrangian

\mathscr{I}_{Gr}:=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}{\mathfrak{L}}_{Gr}

is referred to as the gravitational action, where
\Sigma_{4}\subseteq{\mathcal{}M}
is appropriate closed and oriented subset of the inertial manifold.

The cosmological constant has a physical dimension of
[\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}]=L^{-2}
in our units.
This study sets the Lagrangian form and action integral to null physical dimension
[{\mathfrak{L}}]=[\mathscr{I}]=1
.
Here, fundamental constants
\hbar\kappa
appear associated with the gravitational Lagrangian to keep null physical dimension.
Although the Planck constant appears in the Lagrangian, the theory is still classical.

Remark 4.2
.

The gravitational Lagrangian defined by
Definition
4.1
is equivalent to the standard Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textrm{EH}}:=\frac{1}{\hbar\kappa}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{R}^{\circ\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ\circ}-\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}\right).

Proof.

The supertrace of the gravitational form squared is provided as

\displaystyle Str[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{Gr}\wedge\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{Gr}]

\displaystyle={\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}}\left[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\wedge\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\right]-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\right]=-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\right].

Theorem 4.2
in Ref.
[
33
]
and
Remark 2.1
in Ref.
[
34
]
show that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptc\hskip-0.40974pt\mathrm{P}}}\right]=\frac{\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}}}{2}\mathfrak{R}^{{\circ}\star}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}_{\star{\circ}}=-\frac{\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}}}{2}\mathfrak{R}^{{\circ}\star}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\circ}\star},

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}Str\left[\mathfrak{F}_{Gr}\wedge\mathfrak{F}_{Gr}\right]=\frac{\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}}}{2}\mathfrak{R}^{\circ\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}_{\circ\circ};

thus,
{\mathfrak{L}}_{Gr}=\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textrm{EH}}
is maintained.
∎

A space of the two-form objects is split into two subspaces owing to the Hodge-dual operator as mentioned in
section
2.4.2
; thus, four-form object
{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}[\mathfrak{R}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}]
is split into two parts such as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}[\mathfrak{R}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}]={\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}[\mathfrak{R}^{+}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}^{+}]+{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}[\mathfrak{R}^{-}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}^{-}].

More precisely, it is provided that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}[\mathfrak{R}^{\pm}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}^{\pm}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(R\pm i\epsilon^{\hskip 5.7361pt{\star\star}}_{{\circ\circ}}R^{{\circ\circ}}_{\hskip 5.7361pt{\star\star}}\right)\mathfrak{v},

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}[\mathfrak{R}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}]

\displaystyle=R\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{v},

where
R
is scalar curvature (
7
).
Coordinate expressions using the trivial basis are

\displaystyle\left[\mathfrak{R}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}\right]^{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\eta_{\star\star}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{a\star}_{~~{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\star b}_{~~{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ},

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{\circ\circ\circ\circ}\eta^{~}_{{\star\star}}\hskip 1.99997ptR^{a\star}_{~~{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\star b}_{~~{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{v}.

(102)

The torsion-less Riemann-curvature tensor has additional symmetry such that:

\displaystyle\eta_{a{\circ}}\eta_{b{\circ}}R^{{\circ\circ}}_{~~cd}

\displaystyle=\eta_{c{\circ}}\eta_{d{\circ}}R^{{\circ\circ}}_{~~ab},

\displaystyle\textrm{when}~~{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}=0,

and thus, one of
\mathfrak{R}^{\pm}
yields the scalar curvature like

\displaystyle\eta_{\circ\circ}[\mathfrak{R}^{+}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}]^{\circ\circ}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\circ\circ}[\mathfrak{R}^{-}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}]^{\circ\circ}=\frac{1}{2}R\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v},

\displaystyle\textrm{when}~~{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}=0.

The Euler–Lagrange equation of motion requires a torsion-less condition for the Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian; thus, the Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian can be written using only one of the subspaces
V^{\pm}(\Omega^{2})
.
When we choose SD curvature
\mathfrak{R}^{+}
(accordingly with
{\mathfrak{w}}^{+}
) to construct the Lagrangian, the gravitational Lagrangian is represented as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{Gr}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}}}{\hbar\kappa}\left(\mathfrak{R}^{+}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}^{+}-\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathfrak{v}\right),

\displaystyle\textrm{when}~~{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}=0.

We note that the volume form can be written using one of
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\pm}
such that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\mathfrak{v}

\displaystyle=-\eta_{\circ\circ}\left[{\mathfrak{S}}^{+}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}^{+}\right]^{\circ\circ}=\eta_{\circ\circ}\left[{\mathfrak{S}}^{-}\wedge{\mathfrak{S}}^{-}\right]^{\circ\circ}.

We note that
\Omega^{4}\ni{\mathfrak{a}}\propto{\mathfrak{b}}^{\pm}\wedge{\mathfrak{b}}^{\pm}
for
{\mathfrak{b}}\in\Omega^{2}
for any four-form objects due to
{\mathfrak{b}}^{\pm}\wedge{\mathfrak{b}}^{\mp}=0
.

The gravitational Lagrangian is invariant under the general coordinate transformation and the co-Poincaré transformation
[
33
]
.
In the inertial manifold, the number of independent components of the Lagrangian form is 10. Six components are from the curvature two-form, and four are from the vierbein form.
These degrees of freedom correspond to the total degree of freedom for the Poincaré group.
In reality, the Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian is given using only one of the SD- or ASD curvature; thus, only three components of the Lorentz connection are utilised in the Lagrangian.
The Lagrangian form is now treated as a function with two independent forms
({\mathfrak{w}}^{ab},{\mathfrak{e}}^{c})
and is applied to a variational operator independently.
This method is commonly known as the Palatini method
[
40
,
41
]
.

Equations of motion in a vacuum can be obtained by requiring a stationary condition of the action for the connection and vierbein forms, separately.
From the variation concerning connection form
{\mathfrak{w}}
, an equation of motion is provided as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{T}}^{a}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{b}

\displaystyle=\left(d{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{w}}^{a}_{~{\circ}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\right)\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{b}=0,

(103)

which is referred to as the torsion-less equation.
The torsion-less property is obtained from the solution of the equation of motion rather than being an independent constraint.
This equation of motion includes six independent equations, which is the same as the number of the independent components of
{\mathfrak{w}}
; thus, the connection form is uniquely determined from (
103
) when the vierbein form is given.

Next, taking the variation with respect to the vierbein form, one can obtain an equation of motion as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{a{\circ\circ}{\circ}}\mathfrak{R}^{\circ\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}-\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathfrak{V}_{a}

\displaystyle=0,

(104)

where
\mathfrak{V}_{a}:=\epsilon_{a{\circ}{\circ\circ}}{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}/3!
is a three-dimensional volume form.
Equation (
104
) is referred to as the Einstein equation in a vacuum (those with the Yang–Mills fields are provided in section
4.5
).
The curvature- and vierbein-forms can be uniquely determined up to
GL(4,\mathbb{R})
symmetry by solving equations (
103
) and (
104
) simultaneously.

We note that component representations of (
102
) and (
104
) are provided in the
\theta
-metric space as

\displaystyle(\ref{RSv})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}}}}}{4}\left[{\mbox{\boldmath$\epsilon$}}_{\theta}\right]^{\circ\circ\circ\circ}\eta_{\theta\hskip 0.81949pt{\star\star}}\hskip 1.99997ptR^{a\star}_{~~{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\star b}_{~~{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{v},

\displaystyle(\ref{EoM2})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\left(R_{ab}-\frac{1}{2}R\hskip 1.99997pt\eta_{\theta\hskip 0.81949ptab}+\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\hskip 1.99997pt\eta_{\theta\hskip 0.81949ptab}\right)\mathfrak{v}.

4.2
Yang–Mills action

The Yang–Mills Lagrangian consists of spinor field
\psi
, gauge connection
{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
, and dual curvature
\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
.
The spinor field represents a charge distribution of fermionic matter fields in classical mechanics.
The gauge field represents a potential function as a source of force fields such as electromagnetic, weak, or strong forces.
Their equations of motion govern the dynamics of fields.
Gauge and matter fields contribute to the structure of space-time through their stress-energy tensor.

4.2.1
Matter field

The matter-field Lagrangian-form is defined in the spinor-gauge bundle introduced in section
3.3
using the Dirac operator given in (
84a
).
A mass term is introduced in the secondary superpsace of the Yang–Mills bundle as follows:

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath$\mu$}}^{~}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}

\displaystyle:=\gamma\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mu&0\\
0&\hat{\mu}\end{array}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}\otimes{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}.

Real constants
0\leq\mu,\hat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
are particle masses in physics, and they have a mass dimension
[\mu]=[\hat{\mu}]=M
.
The matter-field Lagrangian density is defined as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{}L}_{M}

\displaystyle:=\overline{{\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\left(i\hskip 1.00006pt{\not\!\mathcal{}D}^{~}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}-\frac{1}{\hbar}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mu$}}^{~}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$\Psi$}}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}.

(105)

Physical constant
\hbar
is inserted to keep a physical dimension of Lagrangian densities to
[{\mathcal{}L}]=L^{-4}
.

Definition 4.3

(Matter-field Lagrangian and action)
.

The Lagrangian and action integral for the matter field are defined as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{M}:={\mathcal{}L}_{M}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}~~\mathrm{and}~~\mathscr{I}_{M}:=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}{\mathfrak{L}}_{M}.

The matter-field Lagrangian is Hermitian self-adjoint.

4.2.2
Gauge field

The gauge-field Lagrangian is defined by means of a super trace as follows:

Definition 4.4

(Gauge-field Lagrangian and action)
.

The gauge-field Lagrangian and corresponding gauge action are defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}:=-\frac{1}{2}Str[\mathfrak{ym}\wedge\mathfrak{ym}],

\displaystyle~~~\mathscr{I}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}:=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}},

(106)

which is equivalent to the standard definition of the Yang-Mills Lagrangian.

Supertrace of the Yang–mills form squared is provided as

\displaystyle Str[\mathfrak{ym}\wedge\mathfrak{ym}]

\displaystyle={\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\wedge\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right]-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right]=-2\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right],

thus, the gauge-field action is represented using the trivial basis as

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\|\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\hskip 1.00006pt\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\left(\sum_{I=1}^{N}\eta^{{\circ}\star}\eta^{{\circ}\star}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\star\star}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\tau^{I}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\cdot\tau^{I}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right]\right)\mathfrak{v},

which can be verified by direct calculations from the definition (
95
).
This Lagrangian is the same as the standard definition of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian
[
42
,
43
]
.

Remark 4.5
.

The Yang–Mills action with the
{SU(N)}
principal group in the Eudlidean metric space has extremal at the SD- or ASD curvature.

Proof.

The Yang–Mills action has the representation owing to SD and ASD curvatures as

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\|\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\left(\|\mathfrak{F}^{+}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\|^{2}+\|\mathfrak{F}^{-}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\|^{2}\right).

Here, the trace concerning
SU(N)
indices is not explicitly written for simplicity.
On the other hand, the second Chern class is provided as

\displaystyle\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\eta}}}}\hskip 1.00006ptc^{~}_{2}\left(\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\left({\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\wedge\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right]-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right]^{2}\right),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\left(\|\mathfrak{F}^{+}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\|^{2}-\|\mathfrak{F}^{-}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\|^{2}\right).

From the first line to the second line, we use
{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{\hskip 0.81949pt{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\left[\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right]=0
due to
{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\tau_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I}\right]=0
for a
SU(N)
gauge group.
When
\|\mathfrak{F}\|^{2}
is positive definite, equivalently, when the space-time manifold has the
Euclidean
metric, they yield
\mathscr{I}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}\geq 4\pi^{2}\left|{c}_{2}\left(\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\right)\right|
; thus, the remark is maintained.
∎

In the
\theta
-metric space, it is true only when
\theta=1
.
We note that there are solutions to the Yang–Mills equation other than the SD- nor ASD connection.
Sibner, Sibner and Uhlenbeck
[
44
]
reported the
SU(2)
Yang–Mills connections which are not the SD- nor ASD connection in
S^{4}
.

4.3
Full Lagrangian and equation of motion in Yang–Mills theory

In summary, the Yang–Mills Lagrangian and action integral is obtained as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathrm{MYM}}:={\mathfrak{L}}_{M}+{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}~~\textrm{and}~~\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{MYM}}:=\int{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathrm{MYM}},

and it is expressed using the trivial basis as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathrm{MYM}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bar{\psi}\left(i\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\partial_{\circ}-\frac{i}{2}{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}_{\circ}^{\mu}\omega_{\mu}^{~{\star\star}}{S}_{\star\star}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}\tau^{~}_{I}\right)\psi-\frac{1}{\hbar}{\mu}\bar{\psi}\psi\right\}\mathfrak{v}

\displaystyle~+\left\{(\psi,{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}},\mu)\rightarrow(\hat{\psi},{{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}},\hat{\mu})\right\}\mathfrak{v}+\frac{1}{4}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{\circ\circ}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}.

(107)

Terms with dual field
\hat{\psi}
in formula (
107
) represents the magnetic monopole in physics, which is not confirmed experimentally to date.
This section treats only principal filed
\psi
.
Section
5.2
discusses a magnetic monopole in detail.

Remark 4.6

(Yang–Mills equations of motion)
.

Two equations of motion is obtained from the Yang–Mills action such that

\displaystyle\left(i{\not\!d}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}-\frac{\mu}{\hbar}\right)\psi

\displaystyle=0,

(108a)

\displaystyle d_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}-{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\psi\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}

\displaystyle=0.

(108b)

The first equation is known as the Dirac equation, and the second equation is the Yang–Mills equation.

Proof.

The Dirac equation can be easily obtained by taking a variational operation on the Yang–Mills Lagrangian concerning conjugate field
\bar{\psi}
as

\displaystyle\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\delta_{\bar{\psi}}\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{YM}}=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\delta\bar{\psi}\left(i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\psi-{\mu}\psi\right)\hskip 1.00006pt{\widehat{\mathrm{H}}}(1)=0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\psi-{\mu}\psi=0.

For gauge-field
{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
, an equation of motion is provided owing to a variational operation concerning connection
{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
such that:

\displaystyle\delta_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.73616ptS\hskip-0.73616ptU\hskip-0.73616pt}}\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{YM}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\sum_{I}\left((d\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{I}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}-{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{v}\psi)\delta_{K}^{I}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}f^{I}_{~JK}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{I}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\wedge{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{J}\right)\wedge\delta{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{K}

\displaystyle~-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\Sigma_{4}}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\wedge\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\Rightarrow d_{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}={c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\psi\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ},

where
\partial\Sigma_{4}
is the integration boundary in which variation
\delta{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
vanishes.
In above calculations, a relation

\displaystyle\delta_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.73616ptS\hskip-0.73616ptU\hskip-0.73616pt}}\left(\bar{\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\delta{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{K}}\left(\bar{\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I}\psi\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}\right)\delta{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{K},

\displaystyle=\bar{\psi}\left(\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{v}\right)\psi\hskip 1.00006pt\delta{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I}

with

0=\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}({\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I}\wedge\mathfrak{v})=(\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I})\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{v}-{\mathfrak{A}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}^{I}\wedge\left(\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{v}\right)

is used.
∎

An expression of equations (
108b
) using the trivial basis in
{T\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}
is provided as

\displaystyle~\hbar\left(i\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\partial_{\circ}-\frac{i}{2}{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}_{\circ}^{\mu}\omega_{\mu}^{~{\star\star}}{{S}}_{\star\star}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}\tau_{I}\right)\psi={\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\psi,

(109a)

\displaystyle~\left(\eta^{{\circ\circ}}\partial_{\circ}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{~{\circ}a}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta^{{\circ\circ}}f^{I}_{~JK}{\mathscr{A}}^{J}_{~{\circ}}\mathscr{F}^{K}_{~{\circ}a}\right)\tau^{~}_{I}=-{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\eta_{a{\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\psi.

(109b)

In equation (
109b
), information of the curved space-time is included in the component representation of
\mathscr{F}^{I}
provided in (
92
) through a torsion of the space-time manifold.

The Noether’s theorem ensures vanishing divergence such that:

\displaystyle d\left(\bar{\psi}\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\psi\right)=\partial_{\circ}\left(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\circ}\psi\right)=0,

which is referred to as a current conservation in physics.

4.4
Scalar action

This section provides a geometrical construction of the Higgs field.
A scalar field belonging to the fundamental representation of the gauge field is referred to as the Higgs filed in physics.
Higgs field
{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\in C^{\infty}({T\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}})
is introduced as a complex-valued section in the gauge bundle.
The equation of motion for the Higgs field is known as the Klein–Gordon equation and is provided from an action integral using the trivial basis in
{\mathcal{}M}
such as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}_{\textrm{Higgs}}:=-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\left[\eta^{{\circ\circ}}\nabla_{\circ}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\nabla_{\circ}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}-V\left(\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|\right)\right]\mathfrak{v},

where
\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}={\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}
,
\nabla_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}-i\hskip 1.00006ptc^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\mu}t_{I}
is a
SU(N)
-covariant differential in
{T\hskip-1.99997pt\mathscr{M}}
, and
V\left(\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|\right)
is a
SU(N)
-invariant potential term.

The Dirac equation was originated from a genius idea by Dirac to realize a square-root of Laplace operator
\Box:=\eta^{{\circ\circ}}\partial_{\circ}\partial_{\circ}
.
In this study, the Laplacian is defined as a square of the Dirac operator; thus, an action integral of the Higgs field is defined as

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{\textrm{Higgs}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[({\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}){\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}-{{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}}_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}V\left(\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|\right)\right]\mathfrak{v}.

Remark 4.7
.

An action integral of the Higgs field is expressed using the trivial basis as

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{\textrm{Higgs}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\left\{\Box+\frac{R}{4}+c_{s}\partial^{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}_{\circ}+c_{s}^{2}{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}_{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}+c_{s}A_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\right)\omega_{\mu}^{~{\circ\circ}}\right\}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.0pt+\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}\hskip 1.00006ptV\left(\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|\right)-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma_{4}}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathfrak{v}\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{\circ}\left({\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\right]\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}\right).

An Euler–Lagrange equation of motion is provided by requiring a stationary condition on a variational operation concerning the field
{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}
.
The last term does not contribute to an equation of motion owing to the boundary condition.

Proof.

Direct calculations provide the result similar with the Lichnerowicz formula
4
4

4

See, e.g., chapter 1.3 of Ref.
[
45
]
.
such that:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[({\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger})({\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}})\right]

\displaystyle={\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\left(\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}\partial_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}+i\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\right)\left(\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{b}\partial_{b}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}+i\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right)\right],

\displaystyle=-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}\left(\partial_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{b}\partial_{b}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right)+i\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}\left(\partial_{a}\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle~+{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}\partial_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\left(\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{b}\partial_{b}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}+i\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle~+{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[i\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}\partial_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right]-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\left(\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle=-4{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}\partial^{a}\partial_{a}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}-i{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}\left(\partial_{a}\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right]

\displaystyle~-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\left(\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}\right)^{2}\right]\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}+\partial_{\circ}\left({\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}\right]\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}\right).

The second and third terms, respectively, give formulae such that:

\displaystyle{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}^{\dagger}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\left(\partial^{~}_{\circ}\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\diamond}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\diamond}{S}^{\circ\circ}\right]\eta_{{\circ}\star}\eta_{{\circ}\star}\left(\partial^{~}_{\diamond}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\diamond}\omega_{\mu}^{~{\star\star}}\right)\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}+4i\left(c_{s}\partial^{~}_{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}\right)\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2},

\displaystyle=-2i\left(\partial^{~}_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\omega_{\mu}^{~{\circ\circ}}\right)\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}+4ic_{s}\left(\partial^{~}_{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}\right)\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2},

and

\displaystyle-{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}\left[\left(\iota_{{\gamma}_{\hskip-0.73616pts\hskip-0.73616ptp\hskip 0.73616pt}}\mathfrak{O}\right)^{2}\right]\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2}=-2\left({\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\omega_{\mu~\star}^{~{\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathcal{E}}^{\nu}_{\diamond}\omega_{\nu}^{~\star\diamond}+c^{~}_{s}{\mathscr{A}}^{~}_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\omega_{\mu}^{~{\circ\circ}}+2c_{s}^{2}{\mathscr{A}}^{~}_{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{\circ}\right)\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|^{2},

where a
SU(N)
index of
{\mathscr{A}}
is omitted for simplicity.
On the other hand, from the definition of the Lorentz curvature,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}^{ab}

\displaystyle=d{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{w}}^{a}_{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{w}}^{{\circ}b},

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{\mu}\omega_{\nu}^{~ab}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega_{\mu~{\circ}}^{~a}\hskip 1.99997pt\omega_{\nu}^{~{\circ}b}\right)dx^{\mu}\wedge dx^{\nu},

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(\partial_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\omega_{\mu}^{~ab}\right)-\left(\partial_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\right)\omega_{\mu}^{~ab}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\omega_{\mu~\star}^{~a}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathcal{E}}^{\nu}_{\circ}\omega_{\nu}^{~\star b}\right\}{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ},

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{2}R^{ab}_{\hskip 4.91673pt{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ},

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R:=R^{{\circ}\star}_{\hskip 4.91673pt{\circ}\star}=2\left(\partial^{~}_{\circ}\omega_{\circ}^{~{\circ\circ}}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega_{\star}^{~\star{\circ}}\omega_{\diamond}^{~{\circ}\diamond}\eta^{~}_{{\circ\circ}}-\left(\partial^{~}_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\right)\omega_{\mu}^{~{\circ\circ}}\right),

where
\omega_{a}^{~bc}:={\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{a}\omega_{\mu}^{~bc}
.
Finally, by gathering up above results into
\mathscr{I}_{\textrm{Higgs}}
, the remark is maintained.
∎

Consequently, the equation of motion for the Higgs field is provided as the Euler–Lagrange equation such that:

\left(\Box+\frac{R}{4}+\frac{\partial V\left(\bigl|{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\bigr|\right)}{\partial{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}+\eta^{\circ\circ}_{~}\sum_{I}\left(c^{~}_{s}\partial^{~}_{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}+c_{s}^{2}f^{I}_{~JK}{\mathscr{A}}^{J}_{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{K}_{\circ}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}\\
\hskip 5.0pt+\frac{{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}}{2}\left(\partial^{~}_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}+c^{~}_{s}\sum_{I}{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}\right)\omega_{\mu}^{~{\circ\circ}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}}=0,

where
SU(N)
indices are indicated explicitly.
It is clear that this equation is
GL(4,\mathbb{R})
and
SU(N)
invariant owing to its construction.

4.5
Torsion and stress-energy forms of Yang–Mills theory

Yang–Mills field is a source of torsion and curvature of space-time.
The right-hand side of the torsion equation (
103
) and the Einstein equation (
104
) is given by torsion and a stress-energy tensor of the Yang–Mills field, respectively.
They are defined as follows:

Definition 4.8

(torsion and stress-energy forms)
.

Torsion two-form
{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}
and stress-energy three-form
\mathfrak{E}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}
of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian are respectively defined as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}:=\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta{\mathfrak{w}}}-d\left(\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta(d{\mathfrak{w}})}\right)~~\mathrm{and}~~\mathfrak{E}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}:=\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta{\mathfrak{e}}}-d\left(\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta(d{\mathfrak{e}})}\right).

Remark 4.9

(torsion and stress-energy forms of Yang–Mills Lagrangian)
.

For the Yang–Mills Lagrangian, a torsion form is provided as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}^{a}

\displaystyle=\left\{K^{a}_{~b_{1}b_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta^{a}_{b_{1}}K^{\circ}_{~b_{2}{\circ}}-\delta^{a}_{b_{2}}K^{\circ}_{~b_{1}{\circ}}\right)\right\}{\mathfrak{e}}^{b_{1}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{b_{2}},

(110)

where

\displaystyle K^{a}_{~bc}

\displaystyle:=-i\hbar\left(\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}{{S}}^{~}_{bc}\psi\right),

(111)

and the stress-energy form is

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{YM}}]_{a}

\displaystyle=\left\{\hbar\bar{\psi}\left(i\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}\partial_{\circ}-\frac{i}{2}{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}_{\circ}^{\mu}\omega_{\mu}^{~{\star\star}}{{S}}^{~}_{\star\star}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{\mathcal{E}}^{\mu}_{\circ}{\mathscr{A}}^{I}_{\mu}t_{I}\right)\psi-{m_{e}}\bar{\psi}\psi\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\left.-\frac{\hbar}{4}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{\circ\circ}\right\}\mathfrak{V}_{a}.

(112)

Proof.

For the torsion form, a tensor
K
is introduced as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}}

\displaystyle=:K^{\circ}_{~ab}\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}.

Coefficients of tensor
K
can be obtained by direct calculations from (
107
) as

\displaystyle\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta{\mathfrak{w}}^{ab}}=\frac{\delta{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}}{\delta\omega_{\mu}^{~ab}}\partial_{\mu}

\displaystyle=-i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\left(\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{{S}}^{~}_{ab}\psi\right)\frac{1}{3!}\epsilon_{{\circ}\star{\star\star}}{\mathfrak{e}}^{\star}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\star}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\star},

\displaystyle=-i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\left(\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{{S}}^{~}_{ab}\psi\right)\mathfrak{V}_{\circ},

and thus, (
111
) is provided.
Torsion form (
110
) can be obtained by solving algebraic equation

\epsilon_{ab{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{T}}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}^{\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}=K^{\circ}_{~ab}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}.

Energy-stress form
\mathfrak{E}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}
can be obtained by direct calculations from (
107
).
∎

In summary, the Einstein equation and the torsion equation with the Yang–Mills field are respectively provided using the trivial basis in
{T\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{a{\circ\circ}{\circ}}\mathfrak{R}^{\circ\circ}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}-\Lambda_{\mathrm{c}}\mathfrak{V}_{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-[\mathfrak{E}_{{\mathrm{YM}}}]_{a}~\Leftarrow~(\ref{esform}),

(113)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(d{\mathfrak{e}}^{a}+{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptg\hskip-0.81949ptr}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathfrak{w}}^{a}_{~{\circ}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}\right)=\hbar\left(-i\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{a}{{S}}^{~}_{{\circ}\star}\psi+2\delta^{a}_{\hskip 1.63885pt[{\circ}}{\eta}^{~}_{\star]\diamond}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\diamond}\psi\right){\mathfrak{e}}^{{\circ}}\wedge{\mathfrak{e}}^{\star}.

The stress-energy due to the gauge field tensor (corresponding to the last term of the right-hand side of (
112
)) is provided as
T^{ab}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}:=-{\hbar}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta^{ab}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{\circ\circ}/4
.
Whereas the standard form of the stress-energy tensor is traceless, its trace is not zero.
The standard traceless stress-energy tensor can be obtained by adding total derivative term
\hbar\hskip 1.00006ptd({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\wedge\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})
to Lagrangian
{\mathfrak{L}}_{{\textrm{Y}\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{M}}}
.
This additional term gives term
\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\eta_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{a{\circ}}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{b{\circ}}
in the stress-energy tensor, and the standard representation of the stress-energy tensor for the gauge field is obtained as

\displaystyle T^{\overline{ab}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}

\displaystyle:=\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{I}\left(\eta_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{a{\circ}}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{b{\circ}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{ab}\mathscr{F}^{I}_{\circ\circ}\mathscr{F}_{I}^{\circ\circ}\right),

(114)

which is traceless such as
\eta_{{\circ\circ}}T^{{\circ\circ}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}=0
.

5
Index theorem in general relativistic Yang–Mills theory

Both Lagrangians of general relativity and the Yang–Mills theory are defined using the supertrace in the secondary superspace.
According to methods given in section
2.5.1
and
2.6
, this section discussed a topological index in the general relativistic Yang–Mills bundle with Euclidean and Lorentz metrics.

5.1
Chern index of space-time manifold

This section treats the characteristic class of the inertial manifold.
The characteristic class is an element of the cohomology group of the classifying space of the principal bundle.
We utilize the Chren characteristic to classify the inertial manifold.
The first Chern class of the inertial manifold is zero since the curvature
\mathfrak{R}
is traceless concerning tensor coefficients in
{T\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}
.
In the four-dimensional manifold, only the second Chern class can have a non-zero value provided such that:

\displaystyle c^{~}_{2}\left(\mathfrak{R}\right)=p_{1}\left(\mathfrak{R}\right)=\frac{{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}}\left[\mathfrak{R}\wedge\mathfrak{R}\right]}{8\pi^{2}}\in H^{4}({\mathcal{}M},\mathbb{Z})

where
\mathfrak{R}
is provided as a solution of the Einstein equation and the torsion-less condition.
A flat inertial manifold has no singularity, and its domain is the whole
{\mathcal{}M}{\cong}\mathbb{R}^{4}
.
After the one-point compactification of
{\mathcal{}M}
, the Poincaré duality theorem insists that
H^{4}({\mathcal{}M}{\cong}S^{4},\mathbb{Z}){\cong}H_{0}(S^{4},\mathbb{Z}){\cong}\mathbb{Z}
.
The de Sitter space-time, as well as the Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker space-time, has topology
M_{\textrm{dS}}\cong\mathbb{R}{\otimes}S^{3}
, and after the one-point compactification of the time coordinate, it has cohomology such that:

\displaystyle H^{4}(S^{1}{\otimes}S^{3},\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle{\cong}\sum_{i+j=4}H^{i}(S^{1},\mathbb{Z}){\otimes}H^{j}(S^{3},\mathbb{Z}){\cong}H_{0}(S^{1},\mathbb{Z}){\otimes}H_{0}(S^{3},\mathbb{Z}){\cong}\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}.

Here, we use the Künneth theorem and the Poincaré duality theorem.

On the other hand, black hole solutions have a singularity, e.g., the Schwarzschild solution is defined in
\mathbb{R}^{1}{\otimes}\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}
and the Kerr–Newman solution has a domain as
\mathbb{R}^{1}{\otimes}\mathbb{R}^{3}{\setminus}S^{1}
.
The singularity-ring
S^{1}
in the Kerr–Newman solution is homotopically contractible; thus, its homology is the same as the Schwarzschild solution’s one.
Therefore, it is enough to consider the cohomology of the Schwarzschild solution.
An oriented and closed domain of the Schwarzschild solution, denoted as
{\mathcal{}M}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{Schw}}
, is constructed by means of the one-point compactification of
{\mathcal{}M}
as follows:
Time variable
t
is one-point compactified as
\mathbb{R}{\mapsto}S^{1}
.
A three-dimensional spacial manifold without the origin is the closed manifold such that
\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus\{0\}{\cong}S^{3}
; thus, the domain of the Schwarzschild solution is a closed manifold
{\mathcal{}M}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{Schw}}{\cong}S^{1}{\otimes}S^{3}
.
The cohomology of a domain of the Schwarzschild solution is provided as

H^{4}({\mathcal{}M}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\textrm{Schw}},\mathbb{Z}){\cong}\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
.

In summary, the second Chern class of the known solutions of the Einstein equation in the inertial bundle has integral cohomology.
In reality, direct calculations of
{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}_{{T\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathcal{}M}}}[\mathfrak{R}\wedge\mathfrak{R}]
with the
\theta
-metric show

\displaystyle c^{~}_{2}\left(\mathfrak{R}\right)

\displaystyle\propto\eta_{\theta\hskip 0.81949pt{\circ\circ}}\hskip 1.99997pt\eta_{\theta\hskip 0.81949pt{\star\star}}\mathfrak{R}^{{\circ}\star}\wedge\mathfrak{R}^{\star{\circ}}=0,

for all solutions listed above.
Therefore, the Chern characteristic is also zero for the space-time manifold of the known solution of the Einstein equation.

5.2
Index in spinor-gauge bundle

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading superspace of gauge fields consisting of
{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
and
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
induces the corresponding superspace of spinor fields
\psi
and
\hat{\psi}
, respectively.
Whereas a spinor concerning
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
(magnetic monopole) has not been observed experimentally, a dual spinor is introduced as a section in the spinor-gauge bundle.
The second term of Lagrangian (
107
) is kept in theory in this section.
This section treats the flat space-time with
{\mathfrak{w}}=0
.

The magnetic-monopole Lagrangian is provided as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}_{\textrm{MP}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{{\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}}\left(i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{d}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}-\hat{\mu}{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right){{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\right\}\mathfrak{v}.

The second Bianchi identities of gauge and its dual fields are provided as

\displaystyle d^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}=\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}=0.

(115)

Equations of motion for gauge and spinor fields are provided as

\displaystyle d_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}={c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ},

\displaystyle\hat{d}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\mathfrak{F}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}={{c}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ},

(116a)

\displaystyle\left(i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt{\not\!d^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}-\mu{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right){\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}=0,

\displaystyle\left(i\hbar\hskip 1.00006pt{\not\!\hat{d}^{~}_{{\hskip-1.63885pts\hskip-0.81949ptg}}}-\hat{\mu}{\mbox{\boldmath$1$}}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}\right){{\mbox{\boldmath$\hat{\psi}$}}}=0.

(116b)

The equation of motion for
\hat{\psi}
is provided owing to a variational operation on
\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{YM}}
with respect to
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
.
The Bianchi identities (
115
) and equations of motion (
116a
) are generally independent from one another.
The equation of motion for the spinor section in the
\theta
-metric space has an expression using tensor coefficients such that:

\displaystyle-\sqrt{{{\sigma^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949pt\theta}}}}\left(\delta^{I}_{J}\partial_{\circ}+c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}f^{I}_{~JK}[{\mathscr{A}}^{~}_{\theta}]^{K}_{\circ}\right)\left[\mathscr{F}_{\theta}^{J}\right]^{{\circ}a}\tau^{~}_{I}=\left[j^{~}_{\theta}\right]^{a},

(117)

where

\displaystyle\left[\mathscr{F}_{\theta}^{J}\right]^{ab}:=\eta_{\theta}^{a{\circ}}\eta_{\theta}^{b\star}\left[\mathscr{F}_{\theta}^{J}\right]_{{\circ}\star}~~\textrm{and}~~j^{a}:={c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}}\gamma_{\theta}^{a}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}.

We note that both
\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
and
\mathfrak{V}_{\bullet}
are defined using the Levi-Civita tensor; thus, it can be factored out after rearranging indices.
Therefore, the equation of motion for the spinor section is provided with the connection and curvature of the spinor-gauge bundle.

For the
U(1)
gauge, the Bianchi identities correspond to the (so-called) first set of the Maxwell equations, and they are not equations but identities.
The second set of the Maxwell equations corresponds to (
116a
) and govern the dynamics of electric and magnetic fields.
We note that the dual field
\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
is the electromagnetic field created by an electron field
\psi
through the electromagnetic potential
{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}}
in this study.

Example 5.1
.

electric and magnetic poles:

This example treats a topological index of the spinor-gauge bundle with the
U(1)
-gauge group.
Spinor fields
\psi
and
\hat{\psi}
, conventionally called an electron and a magnetic monopole, are referred to as
electric pole
and
magnetic pole
in this study, respectively.
We start a discussion from a Coulomb-type electric-potential of an electric pole in the flat space-time manifold with the
\theta
-metric.
When a Coulomb-type potential in globally flat
\mathscr{M}
is provided as

\displaystyle V(r)=\frac{e^{i\pi\theta/2}}{r}

using a local three-dimensional polar coordinate
\xi^{a}=(t,r,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
.
a connection of the gauge bundle is obtained as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}

\displaystyle:=V(r)\hskip 1.99997ptdt\in\Omega^{1}\left({T^{*}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\setminus\hskip-1.00006pt}\{r=0\}\right).

(118)

A curvature is obtained owing to the structure equation of abelian gauge-group such that:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}:=d{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=\left(\partial_{\circ}V(r)\right)d\xi^{\circ}{\wedge}dt=\frac{e^{i\pi\theta/2}}{r^{2}}\hskip 1.99997ptdt\wedge dr.

(119)

Thus, the dual curvature is provided as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{i\pi\theta/2}}{\sqrt{-e^{i\pi\theta}}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}(rd\theta_{1})\wedge(r\sin{\theta_{1}}d\theta_{2}).

(120)

We note that

\displaystyle\left.\frac{e^{i\pi\theta/2}}{\sqrt{-e^{i\pi\theta}}}\right|_{\theta\rightarrow+0}=\left.\frac{e^{i\pi\theta/2}}{\sqrt{-e^{i\pi\theta}}}\right|_{\theta\rightarrow 1}=i.

We treat the
\theta
-metric space with
0\leq\theta\leq 1
; thus, we write
e^{i\pi\theta/2}/\sqrt{-e^{i\pi\theta}}=i
, hereafter.

Tensor coefficient
\hat{\mathscr{F}}
is defined in
{T^{*}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}
except
r=0
and is static; thus, the domain of
\hat{\mathscr{F}}
is
\Sigma_{\hat{\mathscr{F}}}={T^{*}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\setminus\hskip-1.00006pt}\{r=0\}\cong\mathbb{R}\otimes(\mathbb{R}^{3}{\hskip-1.00006pt\setminus\hskip-1.00006pt}\{r=0\})
.
An equation of motion in the spinor-gauge bundle is provided as

\displaystyle d\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=i\hskip 1.00006ptd\hskip-1.99997pt\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\hskip 1.99997pt(rd\theta_{1})\wedge(r\sin{\theta_{1}}d\theta_{2})\right)={c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}.

(121)

This integration in three-dimensional volume
D^{3}
is performed as

\displaystyle\int_{D^{3}}d\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=\int_{\partial\hskip-0.81949ptD^{3}=S^{2}}\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=i\int_{S^{2}}\sin{\theta_{1}}\hskip 1.00006ptd\theta_{1}\wedge d\theta_{2}=4\pi i.

(122)

The first equality is just a formal relation because
d\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=0
in reality; this is special for the
U(1)
-gauge bundle.
We note that the covariant differential is just the external derivative for an abelian gauge group; thus, the equation of motion is equivalent to the Bianchi identity.

For the right-hand side of (
121
), we obtain a stationary electric-pole solution at the origin.
We integrate it as

\displaystyle{c^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}}\int_{D^{3}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\bar{\hat{\psi}}$}}\gamma_{\hskip-0.81949pts\hskip-0.81949ptp\hskip 0.81949pt}^{\circ}{{\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}}}\hskip 1.99997pt\mathfrak{V}_{\circ}=\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-3.00003ptdr\int_{0}^{\pi}\hskip-3.00003ptrd\theta_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\hskip-3.00003ptr\sin{\hskip-1.00006pt\theta_{1}}d\theta_{2}\hskip 3.00003ptj^{0}=4\pi{i},

where
j^{a}
is a static electron current given as

\displaystyle~j^{0}=4\pi{i}\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{3}({\mbox{\boldmath$r$}})=4\pi{i}\frac{\delta(r)\delta(\theta_{1})\delta(\theta_{2})}{r^{2}\sin{\theta_{1}}},

\displaystyle~j^{1}=j^{2}=j^{3}=0;

thus, it is consistent with (
122
).
A topological invariant of the electric-pole current is provided as the Chern characteristic such that:

\displaystyle ch\left(\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)

\displaystyle=dim\left(U(1)\right)+c_{1}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(c_{1}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)^{2}-2c^{~}_{2}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)\right),

where

\displaystyle dim\left(U(1)\right)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle c_{1}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2\pi}\int_{S^{2}}\textrm{Tr}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}]=-2,

\displaystyle c^{~}_{2}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\int_{D^{4}}\left(\textrm{Tr}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}]-\textrm{Tr}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}]\wedge{\mathrm{Tr}\hskip-0.50003pt}[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}]\right)=0.

We note that
\textrm{Tr}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}[\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}]=\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}
for the
U(1)
gauge group.
Therefore, we obtain the Chern characteristic of the electric pole as
ch(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt})=1
for any
\theta\in[0,1]
.

Figure 3
:

Arrows show three-dimensional vector representation of the dual connection for the Coulomb type static potential.
Vectors do not defined on a
z
-axis in this example.

The dual connection is provided as a solution of the structure equation for the given dual-curvature such that:

\displaystyle d\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}=\frac{i}{r^{2}}(rd\theta_{1})\wedge(r\sin{\theta_{1}}d\theta_{2}).

This equation has a unique solution with boundary condition of
\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\hat{\mathscr{A}}=0
such as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}}=-\frac{i}{r}\cot{\theta_{1}}\hskip 1.99997pt(r\sin{\theta_{1}}d\theta_{2})=-i\cos{\theta_{1}}d\theta_{2},

and it is represented using Cartesian coordinate
\xi^{a}=(t,x,y,z)
as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}}=i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{y\hskip 1.00006ptdx-x\hskip 1.00006ptdy}{x^{2}+y^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{z}{r}\in\Omega^{1}\left({T^{*}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathcal{}M}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\setminus\hskip-1.00006pt}\{x=0\wedge y=0\}\right).

This solution is known as the Dirac monopole, and a one-dimensional manifold subtracted from
\mathbb{R}^{3}
is known as the Dirac string.
Three-dimensional vectors of connection
({\mathscr{A}}_{x},{\mathscr{A}}_{y},{\mathscr{A}}_{z})
at
t=0
are visualized in Figure
3
.
We note that the one-dimensional singularity along the Dirac string is removable, and an essential singularity is a point at
x=y=z=0
.
In reality, the same solution is represented using a cylindrical coordinate
\xi_{\textrm{cyl}}:=(t,\rho,\phi,z)
as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}}=\frac{iz}{\sqrt{\rho^{2}+z^{2}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd\phi;~

\displaystyle\textrm{thus,}~~\lim_{\rho\rightarrow 0}\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{{\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt(1)\hskip-0.81949pt}}\big|_{z\neq 0}\rightarrow{i\hskip 1.00006pt\textrm{sign}(z)d\phi}.

Before calculating the topological index of the dual spinor-gauge bundle, we consider a relation between the spin-gauge connection and its dual; Figure
4
shows a schematic diagram of its structure.
We start from gauge connection
{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
; then, the structure equation concerning
{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
gives gauge curvature
\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
.
The Hodge-dual of the gauge field (curvature) gives the equation of motion in the spinor-gauge bundle such as the left equation in (
116a
).
We note that dual-curvature
\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
is not the curvature corresponding the spinor-gauge connection, and spinor filed
\psi
interacts with gauge connection
{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
.
When we start from the spinor current
j(\psi)
, a solution of equations (
116a
), (
116b
), and the Bianchi identity (
115
) provides a set of fields
({\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt},\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt})
; then, the gauge curvature
\mathfrak{F}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
is provided as the Hodge-dual of
\hat{\mathfrak{F}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
that fulfils the structure equation with dual connection
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
.
The equation of motion of the (dual-)spinor field has a tensor-coefficient representation owing to the (dual-)connection and (dual-)curvature, respectively, e.g., as shown in (
117
).
Dual connection
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{~}_{\hskip-0.81949ptS\hskip-0.81949ptU\hskip-0.81949pt}
